1	% Section 1: Introduction (compressed, 669→~550 words)
2	
3	\section{Introduction}
4	\label{sec:introduction}
5	
6	Large language models (LLMs) are increasingly used for information extraction (IE)~\cite{li2023codeie,sainz2024gollie,wei2023zeroshot}, but when generating relation triples $(h, r, t)$, neither entity necessarily carries a type consistent with the ontology's domain/range constraints for $r$.
7	We call such outputs \emph{domain/range violations}: structurally valid triples that are ontologically invalid.
8	Violations persist across the capability spectrum: 15.4\% of triples on SciERC~\cite{luan2018scierc} and 18.5\% on DocRED~\cite{yao2019docred} under zero-shot decoding, and 0.5--2\% even after LoRA fine-tuning; rates follow an observed pattern consistent with schema restrictiveness (few valid type pairs per relation) rather than ontology or model scale.
9	At competitive quality (Relation F1 (R-F1) 40--46\%), even 1--2\% residual violations compound across thousands of documents into systematic knowledge graph corruption, making reliable constraint enforcement necessary.
10	
```

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01PRtw3prNppX3y6s7skJp7U`</summary>

```text
10	
11	Existing approaches leave a fundamental gap.
12	Schema-informed prompting reduces violations by 62\% on SciERC but is per-relation unreliable (violations for \textsc{Evaluate-for} actually \emph{increase} by 5.8\,pp).
13	Grammar-constrained decoding~\cite{dong2025xgrammar,willard2023outlines,beurer-kellner2023lmql} ensures syntactic well-formedness but cannot encode cross-field semantic constraints.
14	Post-hoc filtering discards up to 74\% of predictions including true positives (Appendices~\ref{app:cross-model-zs},~\ref{app:posthoc-extended}).
15	Training-based approaches~\cite{sainz2024gollie,lu2022uie} internalize schema knowledge but provide only soft guidance without runtime enforcement.
16	
17	We propose \textbf{OntoCoDec}, a framework that translates IE ontologies into composable layers of decoding-time constraints.
18	A decidability analysis of IE type constraints (\S\ref{sec:decidability}) yields a two-layer architecture: the \emph{grammar layer} compiles ontology constraints into context-free grammars (CFGs) (JSON structure, vocabulary membership) via XGrammar; the \emph{semantic layer} interprets type constraints at runtime via dynamic logit masking over an entity registry, with a scale-dependent layer interaction: at 8B, the semantic layer achieves only 24\% independent DR reduction (near-complete when combined), while at 70B it reaches 84\% independently.
19	OntoCoDec compiles constraints automatically from IE ontologies, requires no constraint-specific training, and is applicable to autoregressive LMs with logits access; we validate on three English datasets (up to 96 relation types), three 8B-class models across three tokenizer families, with 70B-class validation (\S\ref{sec:70b-results}).
20	LoRA + greedy S1 reduces DR to near-zero: DR\,$\leq$\,0.21\% at R-F1 40--46\% ($\pm$0.3\,pp, $+$10\% latency); 97.6--100\% of removed triples are false positives (\S\ref{sec:downstream-impact}). Beam ($k{=}4$) eliminates all violation types at 8B on both RE datasets (${\sim}$1.5$\times$ latency).
21	DR enforcement converts ontologically invalid violations to individually valid but type-inconsistent ones (CF\,$\leq$\,1.4\% fine-tuned); zero-shot DR drops 76--99\% on SciERC and 42--90\% on DocRED, with total violations (DR+CF) decreasing 30--51\% (Appendix~\ref{app:cross-model-zs}).
22	
23	OntoCoDec is a constraint enforcement method, orthogonal and complementary to training-based schema-aware approaches such as GoLLIE~\cite{sainz2024gollie} and KnowCoder~\cite{knowcoder_acl2024}; it can be composed with any generative IE system to improve schema compliance without retraining.
24	
```

Beam ($k{=}4$) eliminates all violation types at 8B on both RE datasets (${\\sim}$1.5$\\times$ latency).\nDR enforcement converts ontologically invalid violations to individually valid but type-inconsistent ones (CF\\,$\\leq$\\,1.4\\% fine-tuned); zero-shot DR drops 76--99\\% on SciERC and 42--90\\% on DocRED, with total violations (DR+CF) decreasing 30--51\\% (Appendix~\\ref{app:cross-model-zs}).\n\nOntoCoDec is a constraint enforcement method, orthogonal and complementary to training-based schema-aware approaches such as GoLLIE~\\cite{sainz2024gollie} and KnowCoder~\\cite{knowcoder_acl2024}; it can be composed with any generative IE system to improve schema compliance without retraining.\n\nOur contributions:\n\\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt]\n    \\item An empirical finding of non-additive layer interaction: grammar-only constraints reduce domain-range violations by 3\\% and semantic-only by 24\\%, but their composition achieves 94\\%---revealing an enabling dependency where grammar-enforced vocabulary membership creates the clean input the semantic layer requires.\n    \\item A two-layer constrained decoding framework showing that domain/range type constraints require stateful enforcement beyond context-free grammars---grammar-only approaches face state spaces of $6^{17}$--$6^{19}$ for typical IE schemas (Proposition~1; \\S\\ref{sec:decidability}). Constraints compile automatically from IE ontologies, validated on schemas with up to 96 relation types.\n    \\item Evidence of scale-dependent layer interaction on Llama-3.1-70B (AWQ INT4): the semantic layer alone achieves 84\\% DR reduction at 70B vs.\\ 24\\% at 8B (\\S\\ref{sec:70b-results}). At 8B, beam $k{=}2$ is Pareto-optimal (R-F1 44.60, CF 0.11\\%, 1.18$\\times$ greedy S1 latency); $k{=}4$ achieves DR${=}$0\\% with near-zero CF at 1.39$\\times$.\n\\end{enumerate}\n",

76	Table~\ref{tab:scierc-ablation} establishes that DR violations are pervasive (15--18\% across datasets) and that constraint layers interact non-additively: grammar-only achieves 3\% DR reduction on SciERC, semantic-only 24\%, but their composition yields 94\%---the \emph{empirical enabling dependency}.
77	On DocRED (96 relations, $\bar{\kappa}{=}0.842$), grammar-only \emph{increases} DR via \textit{latent violation surfacing}: forcing out-of-vocabulary (OOV)$\to$valid relation mapping exposes violations previously masked by vocabulary errors (Appendices~\ref{app:cross-model-zs},~\ref{app:docred-zs-ablation}).
78	Cross-model validation (3 models $\times$ 2 datasets; Table~\ref{tab:cross-model}) confirms DR drops of 76--99\% on SciERC and 42--90\% on DocRED, with R-F1 improving in 5/6 settings; post-hoc filtering achieves DR${=}$0\% but at 15--74\% rejection rates.
79	DR-to-CF conversion represents a severity downgrade: DR is irrecoverable without re-extraction, while CF---individually valid triples with cross-triple inconsistency---is amenable to post-hoc resolution (type consistency reranking (TCR) eliminates all CF in our experiments, with bounded DR regression; Appendix~\ref{app:tcr}).
80	Zero-shot evaluation thus establishes violation prevalence and constraint interaction; fine-tuning demonstrates practical elimination.

phase2_scierc_full_s1: DR=0.0093\nTable~\\ref{tab:scierc-ablation} establishes that DR violations are pervasive (15--18\\% across datasets) and that constraint layers interact non-additively: grammar-only achieves 3\\% DR reduction on SciERC, semantic-only 24\\%, but their composition yields 94\\%---the \\emph{empirical enabling dependency}.\nOn DocRED (96 relations, $\\bar{\\kappa}{=}0.842$), grammar-only \\emph{increases} DR via \\textit{latent violation surfacing}: forcing out-of-vocabulary (OOV)$\\to$valid relation mapping exposes violations previously masked by vocabulary errors (Appendices~\\ref{app:cross-model-zs},~\\ref{app:docred-zs-ablation}).\nCross-model validation (3 models $\\times$ 2 datasets; Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model}) confirms DR drops of 76--99\\% on SciERC and 42--90\\% on DocRED, with R-F1 improving in 5/6 settings; post-hoc filtering achieves DR${=}$0\\% but at 15--74\\% rejection rates.\nDR-to-CF conversion represents a severity downgrade: DR is irrecoverable without re-extraction, while CF---individually valid triples with cross-triple inconsistency---is amenable to post-hoc resolution (type consistency reranking (TCR) eliminates all CF in our experiments, with bounded DR regression; Appendix~\\ref{app:tcr}).\nZero-shot evaluation thus establishes violation prevalence and constraint interaction; fine-tuning demonstrates practical elimination.\n\n% ──────────────────────────────────────────\n%  (e) Composability with Fine-Tuning (expanded)\n% ──────────────────────────────────────────\n\\paragraph{Composability with fine-tuning.}\n\\label{sec:composability}\n% exp_ids: bootstrap_ci_ft_composability.json, w5_lora_scierc_*, w8_*\nFine-tuning alone reduces DR to 0.5--2\\% but leaves residual violations on tight-constraint relations (Table~\\ref{tab:composability}); adding S1 yields DR\\,$\\leq$\\,0.21\\% with no significant $\\Delta$R-F1 (Bonferroni-corrected; 95\\% CIs $\\subseteq [-0.69, {+}0.25]$\\,pp; removed triples are 97.6--100\\% false positives; Appendix~\\ref{app:downstream-impact}).\nUnder fine-tuning, grammar becomes a no-op ($\\Delta$DR$<$0.2\\,pp), making the semantic layer the sole DR defense (41--69\\% on SciERC, 2--75\\% on DocRED; Appendix~\\ref{app:ft-ablation}); three relations with $\\kappa \\geq 0.36$ retain DR, all eliminated by S1 (Appendix~\\ref{app:lora-per-relation}).\nFallback frequency is model-dependent: Qwen triggers 0 fallbacks under LoRA + S1 on SciERC, while Llama triggers 5{,}882 (Table~\\ref{tab:ft-ablation}), reflecting weaker entity typing; despite this, DR reaches 0.09\\% on SciERC (0.21\\% on DocRED), confirming that even frequent fallback does not compromise enforcement (Appendix~\\ref{app:llama-per-relation}).\nCF drops from $+$9.82\\,pp (zero-shot) to $+$1.18\\,pp (fine-tuned), concentrated in \\textsc{Generic}$\\leftrightarrow$X ambiguity (63.3\\% of zero-shot CF; Appendix~\\ref{app:cf-distribution}); beam ($k{=}4$) eliminates all violation types at 8B (R-F1 43--46; \\S\\ref{sec:beam-results}).\n\n% ──────────────────────────────────────────\n%  CF quantification (MF1 response)\n% ──────────────────────────────────────────\n\\paragraph{Cross-field violation analysis.}\n% exp_ids: phase2_scierc_full_s1 (ZS: CF=248, 167 docs), w5_lora_scierc_full_s1_eval (FT: CF=21, 20 docs)\nUnder zero-shot greedy S1, CF is the sole residual violation type (DR, RV, ET all eliminated): 248 cross-field violations arise across 167 documents, of which 63.3\\% involve \\textsc{Generic}$\\leftrightarrow$X type ambiguity (Appendix~\\ref{app:cf-distribution}), concentrated in \\textsc{Generic}$\\leftrightarrow$\\textsc{OtherScientificTerm} (34.7\\%) and \\textsc{Generic}$\\leftrightarrow$\\textsc{Material} (12.3\\%).\nA source breakdown (Appendix~\\ref{app:cascade-analysis}) shows 58.1\\% originate from hallucinated entities (a model capacity issue), while true cascade errors---where first-seen registration locks an incorrect type---account for only 7.3\\%; correct locks are 2.5$\\times$ more frequent, confirming first-seen registration as a net-positive mechanism.\nFine-tuning reduces CF to 21 cross-field violation instances across 20 documents (1.18\\%), with cascade errors completely eliminated (0/21).\nBeam search ($k{=}4$) eliminates all remaining CF at 1.39$\\times$ latency (\\S\\ref{sec:beam-results}).\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\setlength{\\tabcolsep}{3pt}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}lll rrrr r@{}}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Model} & \\textbf{Dataset} & \\textbf{Mode}\n  & \\textbf{DR\\,$\\downarrow$} & \\textbf{CF}\n  & \\textbf{R-F1\\,$\\uparrow$} & \\textbf{E-F1\\,$\\uparrow$}\n  & \\textbf{$\\Delta$DR} \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multirow{4}{*}{Qwen3-8B}\n  & \\multirow{2}{*}{SciERC}\n    & Unconstr.\\ &  1.95 & 0    & 43.86 & 67.05 & --- \\\\\n  & & S1    & \\textbf{0} & 1.18 & 43.77 & 67.05 & $\\downarrow$100\\% \\\\\n\\cmidrule(l){2-8}\n  & \\multirow{2}{*}{DocRED}\n    & Unconstr.\\ &  0.76 & 0.12 & 40.86 & 82.15 & --- \\\\\n  & & S1    & 0.01 & 0.41 & 40.56 & 81.43 & $\\downarrow$98.7\\% \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multirow{4}{*}{\\shortstack[l]{Llama\\\\3.1-8B}}\n  & \\multirow{2}{*}{SciERC}\n    & Unconstr.\\ &  1.11 & 0    & 45.45 & 67.76 & --- \\\\\n  & & S1    &  0.09 & 0.46 & 45.47 & 67.76 & $\\downarrow$92\\% \\\\\n\\cmidrule(l){2-8}\n  & \\multirow{2}{*}{DocRED}\n    & Unconstr.\\ &  0.49 & 0.08 & 43.72 & 83.93 & --- \\\\\n  & & S1    &  0.21 & 0.21 & 43.78 & 83.94 & $\\downarrow$57\\% \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}%\n\\caption{Fine-tuning composability: LoRA $\\times$ unconstrained vs.\\ S1 on SciERC (551 docs) and DocRED (998 dev docs).\nF1 (\\%). No $\\Delta$R-F1 is significant (Bonferroni-corrected; details in text); all DR reductions are ($p{<}0.001$).\nE-F1 (entity F1) includes parse-failure documents (Qwen PF (parse failure): 7--8\\%; Llama PF: 2--3\\%).}\n\\label{tab:composability}\n\\end{table}\n\n% ──────────────────────────────────────────\n%  (f) Downstream impact of DR elimination\n%  exp_ids: task_e_lucky_violation_analysis\n% ──────────────────────────────────────────\n\\paragraph{Downstream impact.}\n\\label{sec:downstream-impact}\nConstraint removal affects only 0.24\\% of gold triples (Appendix~\\ref{app:lucky-violations}); error analysis of 514 false negatives confirms only one (0.2\\%) was blocked by constraints, with dominant sources (relation confusion, entity type polysemy) schema-valid and beyond constraint reach (Appendix~\\ref{app:error-analysis}).\nAcross fine-tuned settings, 36 residual DR violations decompose into entity type errors (39\\%), schema coverage gaps (33\\%), and relation/direction errors (28\\%); the irreducible floor is $\\sim$3 violations (Appendix~\\ref{app:residual-dr-analysis}).\n",

phase2_scierc_full_s1: DR=0.0093\nTable~\\ref{tab:scierc-ablation} establishes that DR violations are pervasive (15--18\\% across datasets) and that constraint layers interact non-additively: grammar-only achieves 3\\% DR reduction on SciERC, semantic-only 24\\%, but their composition yields 94\\%---the \\emph{empirical enabling dependency}.\nOn DocRED (96 relations, $\\bar{\\kappa}{=}0.842$), grammar-only \\emph{increases} DR via \\textit{latent violation surfacing}: forcing out-of-vocabulary (OOV)$\\to$valid relation mapping exposes violations previously masked by vocabulary errors (Appendices~\\ref{app:cross-model-zs},~\\ref{app:docred-zs-ablation}).\nCross-model validation (3 models $\\times$ 2 datasets; Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model}) confirms DR drops of 76--99\\% on SciERC and 42--90\\% on DocRED, with R-F1 improving in 5/6 settings; post-hoc filtering achieves DR${=}$0\\% but at 15--74\\% rejection rates.\nDR-to-CF conversion represents a severity downgrade: DR is irrecoverable without re-extraction, while CF---individually valid triples with cross-triple inconsistency---is amenable to post-hoc resolution (type consistency reranking (TCR) eliminates all CF in our experiments, with bounded DR regression; Appendix~\\ref{app:tcr}).\nZero-shot evaluation thus establishes violation prevalence and constraint interaction; fine-tuning demonstrates practical elimination.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig_ablation_waterfall.pdf}\n\\caption{Non-additive layer interaction on SciERC (zero-shot). Grammar-only and semantic-only reductions (3\\% and 24\\%) compose to 94\\%, revealing an enabling dependency.}\n\\label{fig:ablation-waterfall}\n\\end{figure}\n\n% ──────────────────────────────────────────\n%  (e) Composability with Fine-Tuning (expanded)\n% ──────────────────────────────────────────\n\\paragraph{Composability with fine-tuning.}\n\\label{sec:composability}\n% exp_ids: bootstrap_ci_ft_composability.json, w5_lora_scierc_*, w8_*\nFine-tuning alone reduces DR to 0.5--2\\% but leaves residual violations on tight-constraint relations (Table~\\ref{tab:composability}); adding S1 yields DR\\,$\\leq$\\,0.21\\% with no significant $\\Delta$R-F1 (Bonferroni-corrected; 95\\% CIs $\\subseteq [-0.69, {+}0.25]$\\,pp; removed triples are 97.6--100\\% false positives; Appendix~\\ref{app:downstream-impact}).\nUnder fine-tuning, grammar becomes a no-op ($\\Delta$DR$<$0.2\\,pp), making the semantic layer the sole DR defense (41--69\\% on SciERC, 2--75\\% on DocRED; Appendix~\\ref{app:ft-ablation}); three relations with $\\kappa \\geq 0.36$ retain DR, all eliminated by S1 (Appendix~\\ref{app:lora-per-relation}).\nFallback frequency is model-dependent: Qwen triggers 0 fallbacks under LoRA + S1 on SciERC, while Llama triggers 5{,}882 (Table~\\ref{tab:ft-ablation}), reflecting weaker entity typing; despite this, DR reaches 0.09\\% on SciERC (0.21\\% on DocRED), confirming that even frequent fallback does not compromise enforcement (Appendix~\\ref{app:llama-per-relation}).\nCF drops from $+$9.82\\,pp (zero-shot) to $+$1.18\\,pp (fine-tuned), concentrated in \\textsc{Generic}$\\leftrightarrow$X ambiguity (63.3\\% of zero-shot CF; Appendix~\\ref{app:cf-distribution}); beam ($k{=}4$) eliminates all violation types at 8B (R-F1 43--46; \\S\\ref{sec:beam-results}).\n\n% ──────────────────────────────────────────\n%  CF quantification (MF1 response)\n% ──────────────────────────────────────────\n\\paragraph{Cross-field violation analysis.}\n% exp_ids: phase2_scierc_full_s1 (ZS: CF=248, 167 docs), w5_lora_scierc_full_s1_eval (FT: CF=21, 20 docs)\nUnder zero-shot greedy S1, CF is the sole residual violation type (DR, RV, ET all eliminated): 248 cross-field violations arise across 167 documents, of which 63.3\\% involve \\textsc{Generic}$\\leftrightarrow$X type ambiguity (Appendix~\\ref{app:cf-distribution}), concentrated in \\textsc{Generic}$\\leftrightarrow$\\textsc{OtherScientificTerm} (34.7\\%) and \\textsc{Generic}$\\leftrightarrow$\\textsc{Material} (12.3\\%).\nA source breakdown (Appendix~\\ref{app:cascade-analysis}) shows 58.1\\% originate from hallucinated entities (a model capacity issue), while true cascade errors---where first-seen registration locks an incorrect type---account for only 7.3\\%; correct locks are 2.5$\\times$ more frequent, confirming first-seen registration as a net-positive mechanism.\nFine-tuning reduces CF to 21 cross-field violation instances across 20 documents (1.18\\%), with cascade errors completely eliminated (0/21).\nBeam search ($k{=}4$) eliminates all remaining CF at 1.39$\\times$ latency (\\S\\ref{sec:beam-results}).\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\setlength{\\tabcolsep}{3pt}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}lll rrrr r@{}}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Model} & \\textbf{Dataset} & \\textbf{Mode}\n  & \\textbf{DR\\,$\\downarrow$} & \\textbf{CF}\n  & \\textbf{R-F1\\,$\\uparrow$} & \\textbf{E-F1\\,$\\uparrow$}\n  & \\textbf{$\\Delta$DR} \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multirow{4}{*}{Qwen3-8B}\n  & \\multirow{2}{*}{SciERC}\n    & Unconstr.\\ &  1.95 & 0    & 43.86 & 67.05 & --- \\\\\n  & & S1    & \\textbf{0} & 1.18 & 43.77 & 67.05 & $\\downarrow$100\\% \\\\\n\\cmidrule(l){2-8}\n  & \\multirow{2}{*}{DocRED}\n    & Unconstr.\\ &  0.76 & 0.12 & 40.86 & 82.15 & --- \\\\\n  & & S1    & 0.01 & 0.41 & 40.56 & 81.43 & $\\downarrow$98.7\\% \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multirow{4}{*}{\\shortstack[l]{Llama\\\\3.1-8B}}\n  & \\multirow{2}{*}{SciERC}\n    & Unconstr.\\ &  1.11 & 0    & 45.45 & 67.76 & --- \\\\\n  & & S1    &  0.09 & 0.46 & 45.47 & 67.76 & $\\downarrow$92\\% \\\\\n\\cmidrule(l){2-8}\n  & \\multirow{2}{*}{DocRED}\n    & Unconstr.\\ &  0.49 & 0.08 & 43.72 & 83.93 & --- \\\\\n  & & S1    &  0.21 & 0.21 & 43.78 & 83.94 & $\\downarrow$57\\% \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}%\n\\caption{Fine-tuning composability: LoRA $\\times$ unconstrained vs.\\ S1 on SciERC (551 docs) and DocRED (998 dev docs).\nF1 (\\%). No $\\Delta$R-F1 is significant (Bonferroni-corrected; details in text); all DR reductions are ($p{<}0.001$).\nE-F1 (entity F1) includes parse-failure documents (Qwen PF (parse failure): 7--8\\%; Llama PF: 2--3\\%).}\n\\label{tab:composability}\n\\end{table}\n\n% ──────────────────────────────────────────\n%  (f) Downstream impact of DR elimination\n%  exp_ids: task_e_lucky_violation_analysis\n% ──────────────────────────────────────────\n\\paragraph{Downstream impact.}\n\\label{sec:downstream-impact}\nConstraint removal affects only 0.24\\% of gold triples (Appendix~\\ref{app:lucky-violations}); error analysis of 514 false negatives confirms only one (0.2\\%) was blocked by constraints, with dominant sources (relation confusion, entity type polysemy) schema-valid and beyond constraint reach (Appendix~\\ref{app:error-analysis}).\nAcross fine-tuned settings, 36 residual DR violations decompose into entity type errors (39\\%), schema coverage gaps (33\\%), and relation/direction errors (28\\%); the irreducible floor is $\\sim$3 violations (Appendix~\\ref{app:residual-dr-analysis}).\n",

    "oldString": "\\begin{figure*}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\textwidth]{figures/beam_pareto.pdf}\n\\caption{Beam width vs.\\ R-F1 under LoRA fine-tuning. Left: SciERC (Qwen3-8B solid, Llama-3.1-8B dashed; 551 docs). Right: DocRED (Llama-3.1-8B, 200 docs). Bubble area $\\propto$ total violation rate (DR+CF+RV); green rings mark zero-violation configurations. Star: Pareto-optimal ($k{=}2$).}\n\\label{fig:beam-pareto}\n\\end{figure*}\n\n% exp_ids: r16_70b_scierc_zs_s1, r16_70b_docred_zs_s1, 70b_lora_scierc, 70b_lora_docred_200",
    "newString": "% exp_ids: r16_70b_scierc_zs_s1, r16_70b_docred_zs_s1, 70b_lora_scierc, 70b_lora_docred_200",
    "originalFile": "% Runtime overhead (inline paragraph, no subsection header to save space)\n\n\\paragraph{Runtime overhead.}\n\\label{sec:runtime}\nS1 overhead is $+$13.1\\% on SciERC, lower on DocRED due to amortization over longer sequences; under LoRA, $+$10.0\\% with 37\\% lower absolute latency (Appendix~\\ref{app:ft-latency}).\nConstraint compilation is one-time $<$0.5\\,s; scaling benchmarks confirm constant overhead (2--3\\%) up to 200 entity types and 1{,}000 relations (Appendix~\\ref{app:scalability}).\n\n\\paragraph{Beam search compatibility.}\n\\label{sec:beam-results}\n% exp_id: d066_beam_sweep\nWith LoRA, beam search~\\cite{anderson2017guided} eliminates all four violation types at 8B on both SciERC and DocRED; at 70B, beam $k{=}4$ achieves DR${=}$RV${=}$ET${=}$0\\% with one residual CF on SciERC (Appendix~\\ref{app:beam-search}).\nA beam-width sweep ($k \\in \\{1,2,4,8\\}$) on SciERC reveals $k{=}2$ as Pareto-optimal: R-F1 44.60 (highest), CF 0.11\\%, at only 1.18$\\times$ greedy S1 latency; $k{=}4$ achieves CF${=}$0\\% but lower R-F1 (43.60) at 1.39$\\times$ (Figure~\\ref{fig:beam-pareto}; Table~\\ref{tab:beam-sweep}; latencies relative to greedy S1).\nThis yields three deployment tiers: greedy S1 ($+$10\\% latency, DR${\\leq}$0.21\\%, CF${\\leq}$1.4\\%), beam $k{=}2$ (1.18$\\times$, near-zero CF), and beam $k{=}4$ (1.39$\\times$, all violations${\\to}$0\\% at 8B; Figure~\\ref{fig:deployment-tiers}).\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig_deployment_tiers.pdf}\n\\caption{Three deployment tiers trading latency for violation elimination. Beam $k{=}2$ is Pareto-optimal; $k{=}4$ achieves zero violations at 1.39$\\times$ latency.}\n\\label{fig:deployment-tiers}\n\\end{figure}\nPer-beam memory overhead is $O(k \\times |\\text{entities}| \\times |\\text{types}|)$ for independent entity registry copies, negligible at typical document scales.\n\n\\begin{figure*}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\textwidth]{figures/beam_pareto.pdf}\n\\caption{Beam width vs.\\ R-F1 under LoRA fine-tuning. Left: SciERC (Qwen3-8B solid, Llama-3.1-8B dashed; 551 docs). Right: DocRED (Llama-3.1-8B, 200 docs). Bubble area $\\propto$ total violation rate (DR+CF+RV); green rings mark zero-violation configurations. Star: Pareto-optimal ($k{=}2$).}\n\\label{fig:beam-pareto}\n\\end{figure*}\n\n% exp_ids: r16_70b_scierc_zs_s1, r16_70b_docred_zs_s1, 70b_lora_scierc, 70b_lora_docred_200\n\\paragraph{70B-class validation (single-family).}\n\\label{sec:70b-results}\nThe following results constitute a preliminary single-family scale exploration on Llama-3.1-70B-Instruct (Activation-aware Weight Quantization (AWQ) INT4); cross-family verification (e.g., Qwen-72B) remains future work.\nAll 70B experiments use Llama-3.1-70B-Instruct under AWQ INT4 quantization; an 8B ablation confirms constraint enforcement is unaffected by quantization (Appendix~\\ref{app:quantization}).\nZero-shot S1 eliminates DR on both datasets (SciERC 6.41\\%\\,$\\to$\\,0\\%, DocRED 14.98\\%\\,$\\to$\\,0\\%; R-F1 11--13).\n% exp_ids: r16_70b_scierc_zs_ablation (grammar_only, semantic_only, full_s1)\nA four-way ablation reveals scale-dependent layer interaction: semantic-only achieves 84\\% DR reduction vs.\\ 8B's 24\\% (Table~\\ref{tab:scierc-ablation}), though grammar remains necessary for RV elimination and CF control (semantic-only CF${=}$76).\n% exp_ids: eval_70b_lora_scierc_s1_constrained, eval_70b_lora_docred_200_s1_constrained\nLoRA confirms composability: DR\\,$\\to$\\,0\\% on both datasets (R-F1 44.53, 43.95; $|\\Delta| \\leq 0.20$\\,pp; CF 0.18--1.33\\%); cross-family generalization at 70B scale remains to be verified.\n% exp_ids: eval_70b_lora_docred_200_beam4_s1\nBeam ($k{=}4$) reduces DocRED parse failures (12.5\\%\\,$\\to$\\,0.5\\%, R-F1 $+$4.19\\,pp); SciERC controlled comparison isolates pure beam gain at $+$0.28\\,pp (Appendices~\\ref{app:70b-ablation},~\\ref{app:70b-docred-zs},~\\ref{app:70b-docred}).\n\n% exp_ids: trie_qwen_ft_scierc, trie_qwen_zs_scierc, trie_llama_ft_scierc, trie_llama_zs_scierc\n\\paragraph{Comparison with grammar-constrained approaches.}\n\\label{sec:constrained-baselines}\nOur grammar-only ablation (Table~\\ref{tab:scierc-ablation}, row~2) implements the same constraints as GCD~\\cite{geng2023gcd} and BoostCD~\\cite{boostcd2025}; vLLM \\texttt{guided\\_json}~\\cite{kwon2023vllm} confirms equivalence on Qwen3-8B (DR${=}$15.26\\%; other models use the same backend; Appendix~\\ref{app:gcd-comparison}).\nThe ${\\sim}$15\\% residual DR is the gap our semantic layer addresses (94\\% total reduction); trie-only constraints without JSON enforcement yield 9--51\\% parse failure (Appendix~\\ref{app:trie-baseline}).\n",

S1/S2 = single/two-pass (\\S\\ref{sec:strategies}).\nGrammar = grammar-constrained decoding (GCD) ceiling~\\cite{geng2023gcd}.\n$\\dagger$vLLM \\texttt{guided\\_json}~\\cite{kwon2023vllm} (\\S\\ref{sec:constrained-baselines}).\nDeterministic (greedy, $T{=}$0); robustness evidence in Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model} and Appendices~\\ref{app:bootstrap-effect-sizes},~\\ref{app:prompt-sensitivity},~\\ref{app:doc-length}.}\n\\label{tab:scierc-ablation}\n\\end{table}\n\n% ──────────────────────────────────────────\n%  (a) Zero-shot diagnostic (compressed)\n% ──────────────────────────────────────────\n\\paragraph{Zero-shot diagnostic.}\n% phase2_scierc_unconstrained: DR=0.1537; phase2_scierc_full_s1: DR=0.0093\nTable~\\ref{tab:scierc-ablation} establishes that DR violations are pervasive (15--18\\% across datasets) and that constraint layers interact non-additively: grammar-only achieves 3\\% DR reduction on SciERC, semantic-only 24\\%, but their composition yields 94\\%---the \\emph{empirical enabling dependency}.\nOn DocRED (96 relations, $\\bar{\\kappa}{=}0.842$), grammar-only \\emph{increases} DR via \\textit{latent violation surfacing}: forcing out-of-vocabulary (OOV)$\\to$valid relation mapping exposes violations previously masked by vocabulary errors (Appendices~\\ref{app:cross-model-zs},~\\ref{app:docred-zs-ablation}).\nCross-model validation (3 models $\\times$ 2 datasets; Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model}) confirms DR drops of 76--99\\% on SciERC and 42--90\\% on DocRED, with R-F1 improving in 5/6 settings; post-hoc filtering achieves DR${=}$0\\% but at 15--74\\% rejection rates.\nDR-to-CF conversion represents a severity downgrade: DR is irrecoverable without re-extraction, while CF---individually valid triples with cross-triple inconsistency---is amenable to post-hoc resolution (type consistency reranking (TCR) eliminates all CF in our experiments, with bounded DR regression; Appendix~\\ref{app:tcr}).\nZero-shot evaluation thus establishes violation prevalence and constraint interaction; fine-tuning demonstrates practical elimination.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig_ablation_waterfall.pdf}\n\\caption{Non-additive layer interaction on SciERC (zero-shot). Grammar-only and semantic-only reductions (3\\% and 24\\%) compose to 94\\%, revealing an enabling dependency.}\n\\label{fig:ablation-waterfall}\n\\end{figure}\n\n% ──────────────────────────────────────────\n%  (e) Composability with Fine-Tuning (expanded)\n% ──────────────────────────────────────────\n\\paragraph{Composability with fine-tuning.}\n\\label{sec:composability}\n% exp_ids: bootstrap_ci_ft_composability.json, w5_lora_scierc_*, w8_*\nFine-tuning alone reduces DR to 0.5--2\\% but leaves residual violations on tight-constraint relations (Table~\\ref{tab:composability}); adding S1 yields DR\\,$\\leq$\\,0.21\\% with no significant $\\Delta$R-F1 (Bonferroni-corrected; 95\\% CIs $\\subseteq [-0.69, {+}0.25]$\\,pp; removed triples are 97.6--100\\% false positives; Appendix~\\ref{app:downstream-impact}).\nUnder fine-tuning, grammar becomes a no-op ($\\Delta$DR$<$0.2\\,pp), making the semantic layer the sole DR defense (41--69\\% on SciERC, 2--75\\% on DocRED; Appendix~\\ref{app:ft-ablation}); three relations with $\\kappa \\geq 0.36$ retain DR, all eliminated by S1 (Appendix~\\ref{app:lora-per-relation}).\nFallback frequency is model-dependent: Qwen triggers 0 fallbacks under LoRA + S1 on SciERC, while Llama triggers 5{,}882 (Table~\\ref{tab:ft-ablation}), reflecting weaker entity typing; despite this, DR reaches 0.09\\% on SciERC (0.21\\% on DocRED), confirming that even frequent fallback does not compromise enforcement (Appendix~\\ref{app:llama-per-relation}).\nCF drops from $+$9.82\\,pp (zero-shot) to $+$1.18\\,pp (fine-tuned), concentrated in \\textsc{Generic}$\\leftrightarrow$X ambiguity (63.3\\% of zero-shot CF; Appendix~\\ref{app:cf-distribution}); beam ($k{=}4$) eliminates all violation types at 8B (R-F1 43--46; \\S\\ref{sec:beam-results}).\n\n% ──────────────────────────────────────────\n%  CF quantification (MF1 response)\n% ──────────────────────────────────────────\n\\paragraph{Cross-field violation analysis.}\n% exp_ids: phase2_scierc_full_s1 (ZS: CF=248, 167 docs), w5_lora_scierc_full_s1_eval (FT: CF=21, 20 docs)\nUnder zero-shot greedy S1, CF is the sole residual violation type (DR, RV, ET all eliminated): 248 cross-field violations arise across 167 documents, of which 63.3\\% involve \\textsc{Generic}$\\leftrightarrow$X type ambiguity (Appendix~\\ref{app:cf-distribution}), concentrated in \\textsc{Generic}$\\leftrightarrow$\\textsc{OtherScientificTerm} (34.7\\%) and \\textsc{Generic}$\\leftrightarrow$\\textsc{Material} (12.3\\%).\nA source breakdown (Appendix~\\ref{app:cascade-analysis}) shows 58.1\\% originate from hallucinated entities (a model capacity issue), while true cascade errors---where first-seen registration locks an incorrect type---account for only 7.3\\%; correct locks are 2.5$\\times$ more frequent, confirming first-seen registration as a net-positive mechanism.\nFine-tuning reduces CF to 21 cross-field violation instances across 20 documents (1.18\\%), with cascade errors completely eliminated (0/21).\nBeam search ($k{=}4$) eliminates all remaining CF at 1.39$\\times$ latency (\\S\\ref{sec:beam-results}).\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\setlength{\\tabcolsep}{3pt}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}lll rrrr r@{}}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Model} & \\textbf{Dataset} & \\textbf{Mode}\n  & \\textbf{DR\\,$\\downarrow$} & \\textbf{CF}\n  & \\textbf{R-F1\\,$\\uparrow$} & \\textbf{E-F1\\,$\\uparrow$}\n  & \\textbf{$\\Delta$DR} \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multirow{4}{*}{Qwen3-8B}\n  & \\multirow{2}{*}{SciERC}\n    & Unconstr.\\ &  1.95 & 0    & 43.86 & 67.05 & --- \\\\\n  & & S1    & \\textbf{0} & 1.18 & 43.77 & 67.05 & $\\downarrow$100\\% \\\\\n\\cmidrule(l){2-8}\n  & \\multirow{2}{*}{DocRED}\n    & Unconstr.\\ &  0.76 & 0.12 & 40.86 & 82.15 & --- \\\\\n  & & S1    & 0.01 & 0.41 & 40.56 & 81.43 & $\\downarrow$98.7\\% \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multirow{4}{*}{\\shortstack[l]{Llama\\\\3.1-8B}}\n  & \\multirow{2}{*}{SciERC}\n    & Unconstr.\\ &  1.11 & 0    & 45.45 & 67.76 & --- \\\\\n  & & S1    &  0.09 & 0.46 & 45.47 & 67.76 & $\\downarrow$92\\% \\\\\n\\cmidrule(l){2-8}\n  & \\multirow{2}{*}{DocRED}\n    & Unconstr.\\ &  0.49 & 0.08 & 43.72 & 83.93 & --- \\\\\n  & & S1    &  0.21 & 0.21 & 43.78 & 83.94 & $\\downarrow$57\\% \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}%\n\\caption{Fine-tuning composability: LoRA $\\times$ unconstrained vs.\\ S1 on SciERC (551 docs) and DocRED (998 dev docs).\nF1 (\\%). No $\\Delta$R-F1 is significant (Bonferroni-corrected; details in text); all DR reductions are ($p{<}0.001$).\nE-F1 (entity F1) includes parse-failure documents (Qwen PF (parse failure): 7--8\\%; Llama PF: 2--3\\%).}\n\\label{tab:composability}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig_composability_bar.pdf}\n\\caption{DR violation rates before and after S1 enforcement under LoRA fine-tuning across two models and two datasets.}\n\\label{fig:composability-bar}\n\\end{figure}\n\n% ──────────────────────────────────────────\n%  (f) Downstream impact of DR elimination\n%  exp_ids: task_e_lucky_violation_analysis\n% ──────────────────────────────────────────\n\\paragraph{Downstream impact.}\n\\label{sec:downstream-impact}\nConstraint removal affects only 0.24\\% of gold triples (Appendix~\\ref{app:lucky-violations}); error analysis of 514 false negatives confirms only one (0.2\\%) was blocked by constraints, with dominant sources (relation confusion, entity type polysemy) schema-valid and beyond constraint reach (Appendix~\\ref{app:error-analysis}).\nAcross fine-tuned settings, 36 residual DR violations decompose into entity type errors (39\\%), schema coverage gaps (33\\%), and relation/direction errors (28\\%); the irreducible floor is $\\sim$3 violations (Appendix~\\ref{app:residual-dr-analysis}).\n",

### Tool result

_source line 2214 · 2026-05-25T08:02:26.850Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01LQhp25Q9fPV5tk3Kv33kp5`</summary>

```text
10	where $\wedge$ denotes element-wise AND over the vocabulary $V$; a token is allowed only if it satisfies \emph{both} structural and type-level constraints.
11	The conjunction is asymmetric: $S(t)$ is defined only over $G(t)$. This reflects the scale-dependent layer interaction (\S\ref{sec:method}): the semantic layer requires the relation type to belong to $\mathcal{R}$, a property enforced only by the grammar layer.
12	This dependency is scale-sensitive: 70B semantic-only achieves 84\% DR reduction (vs.\ 24\% on 8B), though grammar remains necessary for RV elimination (\S\ref{sec:70b-results}).
13	
14	\paragraph{Grammar-First Fallback.}
15	When $S(t) \cap G(t) = \emptyset$ (tight constraints leave no token that is both grammar-valid and semantically valid), na\"{\i}ve masking would force selection of a grammar-invalid token, breaking JSON structure.
16	We enforce a \emph{grammar-first fallback}: semantic masking is skipped and the grammar layer alone controls selection.
17	Structural integrity is a hard constraint; type violations are soft errors detectable by downstream validation, with DR (ontologically invalid type pair) more severe than CF (individually valid triples with cross-triple type inconsistency).
18	Extraction tasks are robust to format-level constraints~\cite{tam2024letmespeak}, supporting this asymmetric treatment.
19	
20	\paragraph{Constraint Tightness.}
21	\label{eq:tightness}
22	Per-relation constraint tightness $\kappa(r) = 1 - |\sigma(r)|/|\mathcal{E}|^2$ captures schema restrictiveness; DR reduction correlates descriptively with schema-mean $\bar{\kappa}$ across our three evaluation schemas ($n{=}3$), though entity type confusability is a significant co-factor (Appendix~\ref{app:kappa-case-study},~\ref{app:tcr}).
23	
24	\paragraph{Greedy limitation and fallback cascade.}
25	\label{sec:greedy-limitation}
26	Single-pass per-triple enforcement cannot coordinate type assignments across triples sharing the same entity mention; cross-field consistency requires global inference that exceeds the per-token masking paradigm.
27	The semantic layer commits entity types greedily---an instance of Level~3 (non-local) constraints (\S\ref{sec:decidability})---so CF violations arise when the same entity receives different types across triples; beam search mitigates this (\S\ref{sec:beam-results}).
28	Fallback triggers on 0.5--5.7\% of type-bearing positions (zero-shot), virtually eliminated by fine-tuning ($\leq$\,0.11\%); all residual DR occurs at fallback positions (Appendix~\ref{app:causal-tracing}).
29	
30	\paragraph{Non-rectangularity and independent enforcement.}
31	\label{sec:rectangularity}
32	Type signatures are non-rectangular (0/7 SciERC, 23/96 DocRED rectangular); independent enforcement admits 33--37\% spurious pairs, eliminated by fine-tuning (Table~\ref{tab:composability}).
33	Shuffling type order produces byte-identical outputs across all 551 SciERC documents, confirming order invariance (Appendix~\ref{app:order-invariance}).
34	
```

</details>

    "newString": "Shuffling type order produces byte-identical outputs across all 551 SciERC documents, confirming order invariance (Appendix~\\ref{app:order-invariance}).\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig_composability_bar.pdf}\n\\caption{DR violation rates before and after S1 enforcement under LoRA fine-tuning across two models and two datasets. All reductions significant ($p{<}0.001$).}\n\\label{fig:composability-bar}\n\\end{figure}",
    "originalFile": "% Section: Constraint Composition (compressed, 787→~500 words)\n\nAt each decoding step $t$, let $G(t) \\subseteq V$ and $S(t) \\subseteq V$ denote the tokens permitted by the grammar and semantic layers, respectively.\nThe final mask is their conjunction:\n%\n\\begin{equation}\\label{eq:composition}\n  M_{\\text{final}}(t) = M_{\\text{grammar}}(t) \\;\\wedge\\; M_{\\text{semantic}}(t),\n\\end{equation}\n%\nwhere $\\wedge$ denotes element-wise AND over the vocabulary $V$; a token is allowed only if it satisfies \\emph{both} structural and type-level constraints.\nThe conjunction is asymmetric: $S(t)$ is defined only over $G(t)$. This reflects the scale-dependent layer interaction (\\S\\ref{sec:method}): the semantic layer requires the relation type to belong to $\\mathcal{R}$, a property enforced only by the grammar layer.\nThis dependency is scale-sensitive: 70B semantic-only achieves 84\\% DR reduction (vs.\\ 24\\% on 8B), though grammar remains necessary for RV elimination (\\S\\ref{sec:70b-results}).\n\n\\paragraph{Grammar-First Fallback.}\nWhen $S(t) \\cap G(t) = \\emptyset$ (tight constraints leave no token that is both grammar-valid and semantically valid), na\\\"{\\i}ve masking would force selection of a grammar-invalid token, breaking JSON structure.\nWe enforce a \\emph{grammar-first fallback}: semantic masking is skipped and the grammar layer alone controls selection.\nStructural integrity is a hard constraint; type violations are soft errors detectable by downstream validation, with DR (ontologically invalid type pair) more severe than CF (individually valid triples with cross-triple type inconsistency).\nExtraction tasks are robust to format-level constraints~\\cite{tam2024letmespeak}, supporting this asymmetric treatment.\n\n\\paragraph{Constraint Tightness.}\n\\label{eq:tightness}\nPer-relation constraint tightness $\\kappa(r) = 1 - |\\sigma(r)|/|\\mathcal{E}|^2$ captures schema restrictiveness; DR reduction correlates descriptively with schema-mean $\\bar{\\kappa}$ across our three evaluation schemas ($n{=}3$), though entity type confusability is a significant co-factor (Appendix~\\ref{app:kappa-case-study},~\\ref{app:tcr}).\n\n\\paragraph{Greedy limitation and fallback cascade.}\n\\label{sec:greedy-limitation}\nSingle-pass per-triple enforcement cannot coordinate type assignments across triples sharing the same entity mention; cross-field consistency requires global inference that exceeds the per-token masking paradigm.\nThe semantic layer commits entity types greedily---an instance of Level~3 (non-local) constraints (\\S\\ref{sec:decidability})---so CF violations arise when the same entity receives different types across triples; beam search mitigates this (\\S\\ref{sec:beam-results}).\nFallback triggers on 0.5--5.7\\% of type-bearing positions (zero-shot), virtually eliminated by fine-tuning ($\\leq$\\,0.11\\%); all residual DR occurs at fallback positions (Appendix~\\ref{app:causal-tracing}).\n\n\\paragraph{Non-rectangularity and independent enforcement.}\n\\label{sec:rectangularity}\nType signatures are non-rectangular (0/7 SciERC, 23/96 DocRED rectangular); independent enforcement admits 33--37\\% spurious pairs, eliminated by fine-tuning (Table~\\ref{tab:composability}).\nShuffling type order produces byte-identical outputs across all 551 SciERC documents, confirming order invariance (Appendix~\\ref{app:order-invariance}).\n",

    "originalFile": "% Runtime overhead (inline paragraph, no subsection header to save space)\n\n\\paragraph{Runtime overhead.}\n\\label{sec:runtime}\nS1 overhead is $+$13.1\\% on SciERC, lower on DocRED due to amortization over longer sequences; under LoRA, $+$10.0\\% with 37\\% lower absolute latency (Appendix~\\ref{app:ft-latency}).\nConstraint compilation is one-time $<$0.5\\,s; scaling benchmarks confirm constant overhead (2--3\\%) up to 200 entity types and 1{,}000 relations (Appendix~\\ref{app:scalability}).\n\n\\paragraph{Beam search compatibility.}\n\\label{sec:beam-results}\n% exp_id: d066_beam_sweep\nWith LoRA, beam search~\\cite{anderson2017guided} eliminates all four violation types at 8B on both SciERC and DocRED; at 70B, beam $k{=}4$ achieves DR${=}$RV${=}$ET${=}$0\\% with one residual CF on SciERC (Appendix~\\ref{app:beam-search}).\nA beam-width sweep ($k \\in \\{1,2,4,8\\}$) on SciERC reveals $k{=}2$ as Pareto-optimal: R-F1 44.60 (highest), CF 0.11\\%, at only 1.18$\\times$ greedy S1 latency; $k{=}4$ achieves CF${=}$0\\% but lower R-F1 (43.60) at 1.39$\\times$ (Figure~\\ref{fig:beam-pareto}; Table~\\ref{tab:beam-sweep} in Appendix~\\ref{app:beam-search}; latencies relative to greedy S1).\nThis yields three deployment tiers: greedy S1 ($+$10\\% latency, DR${\\leq}$0.21\\%, CF${\\leq}$1.4\\%), beam $k{=}2$ (1.18$\\times$, near-zero CF), and beam $k{=}4$ (1.39$\\times$, all violations${\\to}$0\\% at 8B; Figure~\\ref{fig:deployment-tiers}).\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig_deployment_tiers.pdf}\n\\caption{Three deployment tiers trading latency for violation elimination. Beam $k{=}2$ is Pareto-optimal; $k{=}4$ achieves zero violations at 1.39$\\times$ latency.}\n\\label{fig:deployment-tiers}\n\\end{figure}\nPer-beam memory overhead is $O(k \\times |\\text{entities}| \\times |\\text{types}|)$ for independent entity registry copies, negligible at typical document scales.\n\n% exp_ids: r16_70b_scierc_zs_s1, r16_70b_docred_zs_s1, 70b_lora_scierc, 70b_lora_docred_200\n\\paragraph{70B-class validation (single-family).}\n\\label{sec:70b-results}\nThe following results constitute a preliminary single-family scale exploration on Llama-3.1-70B-Instruct (Activation-aware Weight Quantization (AWQ) INT4); cross-family verification (e.g., Qwen-72B) remains future work.\nAll 70B experiments use Llama-3.1-70B-Instruct under AWQ INT4 quantization; an 8B ablation confirms constraint enforcement is unaffected by quantization (Appendix~\\ref{app:quantization}).\nZero-shot S1 eliminates DR on both datasets (SciERC 6.41\\%\\,$\\to$\\,0\\%, DocRED 14.98\\%\\,$\\to$\\,0\\%; R-F1 11--13).\n% exp_ids: r16_70b_scierc_zs_ablation (grammar_only, semantic_only, full_s1)\nA four-way ablation reveals scale-dependent layer interaction: semantic-only achieves 84\\% DR reduction vs.\\ 8B's 24\\% (Table~\\ref{tab:scierc-ablation}), though grammar remains necessary for RV elimination and CF control (semantic-only CF${=}$76).\n% exp_ids: eval_70b_lora_scierc_s1_constrained, eval_70b_lora_docred_200_s1_constrained\nLoRA confirms composability: DR\\,$\\to$\\,0\\% on both datasets (R-F1 44.53, 43.95; $|\\Delta| \\leq 0.20$\\,pp; CF 0.18--1.33\\%); cross-family generalization at 70B scale remains to be verified.\n% exp_ids: eval_70b_lora_docred_200_beam4_s1\nBeam ($k{=}4$) reduces DocRED parse failures (12.5\\%\\,$\\to$\\,0.5\\%, R-F1 $+$4.19\\,pp); SciERC controlled comparison isolates pure beam gain at $+$0.28\\,pp (Appendices~\\ref{app:70b-ablation},~\\ref{app:70b-docred-zs},~\\ref{app:70b-docred}).\n\n% exp_ids: trie_qwen_ft_scierc, trie_qwen_zs_scierc, trie_llama_ft_scierc, trie_llama_zs_scierc\n\\paragraph{Comparison with grammar-constrained approaches.}\n\\label{sec:constrained-baselines}\nOur grammar-only ablation (Table~\\ref{tab:scierc-ablation}, row~2) implements the same constraints as GCD~\\cite{geng2023gcd} and BoostCD~\\cite{boostcd2025}; vLLM \\texttt{guided\\_json}~\\cite{kwon2023vllm} confirms equivalence on Qwen3-8B (DR${=}$15.26\\%; other models use the same backend; Appendix~\\ref{app:gcd-comparison}).\nThe ${\\sim}$15\\% residual DR is the gap our semantic layer addresses (94\\% total reduction); trie-only constraints without JSON enforcement yield 9--51\\% parse failure (Appendix~\\ref{app:trie-baseline}).\n",

```text
1	% Runtime overhead (inline paragraph, no subsection header to save space)
2	
3	\paragraph{Runtime overhead.}
4	\label{sec:runtime}
5	S1 overhead is $+$13.1\% on SciERC, lower on DocRED due to amortization over longer sequences; under LoRA, $+$10.0\% with 37\% lower absolute latency (Appendix~\ref{app:ft-latency}).
6	Constraint compilation is one-time $<$0.5\,s; scaling benchmarks confirm constant overhead (2--3\%) up to 200 entity types and 1{,}000 relations (Appendix~\ref{app:scalability}).
7	
8	\paragraph{Beam search compatibility.}
9	\label{sec:beam-results}
10	% exp_id: d066_beam_sweep
11	With LoRA, beam search~\cite{anderson2017guided} eliminates all four violation types at 8B on both SciERC and DocRED; at 70B, beam $k{=}4$ achieves DR${=}$RV${=}$ET${=}$0\% with one residual CF on SciERC (Appendix~\ref{app:beam-search}).
12	A beam-width sweep ($k \in \{1,2,4,8\}$) on SciERC reveals $k{=}2$ as Pareto-optimal: R-F1 44.60 (highest), CF 0.11\%, at only 1.18$\times$ greedy S1 latency; $k{=}4$ achieves CF${=}$0\% but lower R-F1 (43.60) at 1.39$\times$ (Figure~\ref{fig:beam-pareto}; Table~\ref{tab:beam-sweep} in Appendix~\ref{app:beam-search}; latencies relative to greedy S1).
13	This yields three deployment tiers: greedy S1 ($+$10\% latency, DR${\leq}$0.21\%, CF${\leq}$1.4\%), beam $k{=}2$ (1.18$\times$, near-zero CF), and beam $k{=}4$ (1.39$\times$, all violations${\to}$0\% at 8B).
14	Per-beam memory overhead is $O(k \times |\text{entities}| \times |\text{types}|)$ for independent entity registry copies, negligible at typical document scales.
15	
16	% exp_ids: r16_70b_scierc_zs_s1, r16_70b_docred_zs_s1, 70b_lora_scierc, 70b_lora_docred_200
17	\paragraph{70B-class validation (single-family).}
18	\label{sec:70b-results}
19	The following results constitute a preliminary single-family scale exploration on Llama-3.1-70B-Instruct (Activation-aware Weight Quantization (AWQ) INT4); cross-family verification (e.g., Qwen-72B) remains future work.
20	All 70B experiments use Llama-3.1-70B-Instruct under AWQ INT4 quantization; an 8B ablation confirms constraint enforcement is unaffected by quantization (Appendix~\ref{app:quantization}).
21	Zero-shot S1 eliminates DR on both datasets (SciERC 6.41\%\,$\to$\,0\%, DocRED 14.98\%\,$\to$\,0\%; R-F1 11--13).
22	% exp_ids: r16_70b_scierc_zs_ablation (grammar_only, semantic_only, full_s1)
23	A four-way ablation reveals scale-dependent layer interaction: semantic-only achieves 84\% DR reduction vs.\ 8B's 24\% (Table~\ref{tab:scierc-ablation}), though grammar remains necessary for RV elimination and CF control (semantic-only CF${=}$76).
24	% exp_ids: eval_70b_lora_scierc_s1_constrained, eval_70b_lora_docred_200_s1_constrained
25	LoRA confirms composability: DR\,$\to$\,0\% on both datasets (R-F1 44.53, 43.95; $|\Delta| \leq 0.20$\,pp; CF 0.18--1.33\%); cross-family generalization at 70B scale remains to be verified.
26	% exp_ids: eval_70b_lora_docred_200_beam4_s1
27	Beam ($k{=}4$) reduces DocRED parse failures (12.5\%\,$\to$\,0.5\%, R-F1 $+$4.19\,pp); SciERC controlled comparison isolates pure beam gain at $+$0.28\,pp (Appendices~\ref{app:70b-ablation},~\ref{app:70b-docred-zs},~\ref{app:70b-docred}).
28	
29	% exp_ids: trie_qwen_ft_scierc, trie_qwen_zs_scierc, trie_llama_ft_scierc, trie_llama_zs_scierc
30	\paragraph{Comparison with grammar-constrained approaches.}
31	\label{sec:constrained-baselines}
32	Our grammar-only ablation (Table~\ref{tab:scierc-ablation}, row~2) implements the same constraints as GCD~\cite{geng2023gcd} and BoostCD~\cite{boostcd2025}; vLLM \texttt{guided\_json}~\cite{kwon2023vllm} confirms equivalence on Qwen3-8B (DR${=}$15.26\%; other models use the same backend; Appendix~\ref{app:gcd-comparison}).
33	The ${\sim}$15\% residual DR is the gap our semantic layer addresses (94\% total reduction); trie-only constraints without JSON enforcement yield 9--51\% parse failure (Appendix~\ref{app:trie-baseline}).
34	
```

_source line 2270 · 2026-05-25T08:04:04.790Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_015hYLj7AGKJSEGAej8xJfJF`</summary>

```text
1	% Section 7 — Discussion and Conclusion
2	
3	We validated OntoCoDec across 2 RE datasets, 3 model families, and event extraction (\S\ref{sec:wikievents}).
4	The central finding is non-additive layer interaction: grammar alone achieves 3\% DR reduction on SciERC, semantic alone 24\%, but their composition yields 94\% at 8B (\S\ref{sec:scierc-results})---grammar-enforced vocabulary membership creates the clean input the semantic layer requires.
5	
6	\paragraph{Scale-dependent layer interaction.}
7	This dependency weakens at scale: at 70B, the semantic layer independently achieves 84\% DR reduction (\S\ref{sec:70b-results}).
8	We offer a speculative hypothesis: larger models exhibit improved vocabulary calibration, assigning higher probability to ontology-valid tokens and reducing the semantic layer's dependence on grammar-enforced vocabulary membership (Appendix~\ref{app:causal-tracing}); confirming this causal mechanism requires cross-family verification beyond our current scope.
9	Fallback frequency varies dramatically across architectures (0 vs.\ 5{,}882 under LoRA+S1) without affecting enforcement (both DR\,$\leq$\,0.21\%), confirming grammar is necessary but increasingly insufficient as models improve.
10	Constraint enforcement is preserved under INT4 quantization (Appendix~\ref{app:quantization}).
11	
12	\paragraph{DR$\leftrightarrow$CF trade-off.}
13	Greedy S1 reduces DR to $\leq$\,0.21\% but introduces CF (zero-shot $+$9.82\,pp, fine-tuned $+$1.18\,pp)---an architectural consequence of per-triple independent enforcement.
14	Among 248 zero-shot CF violations, 58.1\% involve hallucinated entities (absent from gold) and only 7.3\% are true cascade errors (\S\ref{sec:scierc-results}); fine-tuned CF drops to $\leq$1.4\%.
15	Beam $k{=}2$ reduces CF to 0.11\% at 1.18$\times$ latency (Pareto-optimal; recommended for zero-shot deployment); $k{=}4$ eliminates all violations at 8B (1 residual CF at 70B) at 1.39$\times$ (\S\ref{sec:beam-results}).
16	Net effect: total violations (DR+CF) decrease 30--51\% in zero-shot and 26--52\% under fine-tuned greedy S1; beam $k{=}4$ eliminates all. DR is irrecoverable while CF is amenable to post-hoc resolution (TCR; Appendix~\ref{app:tcr}).
17	
18	\paragraph{Complementarity and deployment.}
19	GoLLIE and KnowCoder internalize schema knowledge via training but lack runtime guarantees; LoRA + S1 shows the two are complementary (DR 1--2\%\,$\to$\,$\leq$0.21\%).
20	S2 (two-pass) degrades at 70B ($-$8\,pp R-F1), consistent with CRANE~\cite{banerjee2025crane}; we recommend S1 as default.
21	
22	The root cause---per-triple enforcement cannot coordinate cross-triple type assignments (Level~3; \S\ref{sec:decidability})---requires global entity-level consensus beyond online masking; beam search and TCR approximate this, with full integration as future work.
23	
```

</details>

  "content": "% Section 7 — Discussion and Conclusion\n\nWe validated OntoCoDec across 2 RE datasets, 3 model families, and event extraction (\\S\\ref{sec:wikievents}).\nThe central finding is non-additive layer interaction: grammar alone achieves 3\\% DR reduction on SciERC, semantic alone 24\\%, but their composition yields 94\\% at 8B (\\S\\ref{sec:scierc-results})---grammar-enforced vocabulary membership creates the clean input the semantic layer requires.\n\n\\paragraph{Scale-dependent layer interaction.}\nThis dependency weakens at scale: at 70B, the semantic layer independently achieves 84\\% DR reduction (\\S\\ref{sec:70b-results}).\nWe offer a speculative hypothesis: larger models exhibit improved vocabulary calibration, assigning higher probability to ontology-valid tokens and reducing the semantic layer's dependence on grammar-enforced vocabulary membership (Appendix~\\ref{app:causal-tracing}); confirming this causal mechanism requires cross-family verification beyond our current scope.\nFallback frequency varies dramatically across architectures (0 vs.\\ 5{,}882 under LoRA+S1) without affecting enforcement (both DR\\,$\\leq$\\,0.21\\%), confirming grammar is necessary but increasingly insufficient as models improve.\nConstraint enforcement is preserved under INT4 quantization (Appendix~\\ref{app:quantization}).\n\n\\paragraph{DR$\\leftrightarrow$CF trade-off.}\nGreedy S1 reduces DR to $\\leq$\\,0.21\\% but introduces CF (zero-shot $+$9.82\\,pp, fine-tuned $+$1.18\\,pp)---an architectural consequence of per-triple independent enforcement.\nAmong 248 zero-shot CF violations, 58.1\\% involve hallucinated entities (absent from gold) and only 7.3\\% are true cascade errors (\\S\\ref{sec:scierc-results}); fine-tuned CF drops to $\\leq$1.4\\%.\nBeam $k{=}2$ reduces CF to 0.11\\% at 1.18$\\times$ latency (Pareto-optimal; recommended for zero-shot deployment); $k{=}4$ eliminates all violations at 8B at 1.39$\\times$ (\\S\\ref{sec:beam-results}).\n\n\\paragraph{Complementarity and deployment.}\nGoLLIE and KnowCoder internalize schema knowledge via training but lack runtime guarantees; LoRA + S1 shows the two are complementary (DR 1--2\\%\\,$\\to$\\,$\\leq$0.21\\%).\nS2 (two-pass) degrades at 70B ($-$8\\,pp R-F1), consistent with CRANE~\\cite{banerjee2025crane}; we recommend S1 as default.\nThe root cause---per-triple enforcement cannot coordinate cross-triple type assignments (Level~3; \\S\\ref{sec:decidability})---requires global entity-level consensus beyond online masking; beam search and TCR approximate this, with full integration as future work.\n"
}

cross-family verification (e.g., Qwen-72B) remains future work.\nAll 70B experiments use Llama-3.1-70B-Instruct under AWQ INT4 quantization; an 8B ablation confirms constraint enforcement is unaffected by quantization (Appendix~\\ref{app:quantization}).\nZero-shot S1 eliminates DR on both datasets (SciERC 6.41\\%\\,$\\to$\\,0\\%, DocRED 14.98\\%\\,$\\to$\\,0\\%; R-F1 11--13).\n% exp_ids: r16_70b_scierc_zs_ablation (grammar_only, semantic_only, full_s1)\nA four-way ablation reveals scale-dependent layer interaction: semantic-only achieves 84\\% DR reduction vs.\\ 8B's 24\\% (Table~\\ref{tab:scierc-ablation}), though grammar remains necessary for RV elimination and CF control (semantic-only CF${=}$76).\n% exp_ids: eval_70b_lora_scierc_s1_constrained, eval_70b_lora_docred_200_s1_constrained\nLoRA confirms composability: DR\\,$\\to$\\,0\\% on both datasets (R-F1 44.53, 43.95; $|\\Delta| \\leq 0.20$\\,pp; CF 0.18--1.33\\%); cross-family generalization at 70B scale remains to be verified.\n% exp_ids: eval_70b_lora_docred_200_beam4_s1\nBeam ($k{=}4$) reduces DocRED parse failures (12.5\\%\\,$\\to$\\,0.5\\%, R-F1 $+$4.19\\,pp); SciERC controlled comparison isolates pure beam gain at $+$0.28\\,pp (Appendices~\\ref{app:70b-ablation},~\\ref{app:70b-docred-zs},~\\ref{app:70b-docred}).\n\n% exp_ids: trie_qwen_ft_scierc, trie_qwen_zs_scierc, trie_llama_ft_scierc, trie_llama_zs_scierc\n\\paragraph{Comparison with grammar-constrained approaches.}\n\\label{sec:constrained-baselines}\nOur grammar-only ablation (Table~\\ref{tab:scierc-ablation}, row~2) implements the same constraints as GCD~\\cite{geng2023gcd} and BoostCD~\\cite{boostcd2025};     "originalFile": "% Runtime overhead (inline paragraph, no subsection header to save space)\n\n\\paragraph{Runtime overhead.}\n\\label{sec:runtime}\nS1 overhead is $+$13.1\\% on SciERC, lower on DocRED due to amortization over longer sequences;     "originalFile": "% Section 5 — SciERC Results (Draft)\n% Data source: Phase 2 SciERC registry experiments\n% exp_ids: phase2_scierc_unconstrained, phase2_scierc_grammar_only,\n%          phase2_scierc_semantic_only, phase2_scierc_full_s1, phase2_scierc_full_s2\n% All numbers verified against registry 2026-05-14.\n\n\\subsection{Ablation and Composability with Fine-Tuning}\n\\label{sec:scierc-results}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:scierc-ablation} presents the five-way ablation on SciERC (Qwen3-8B, zero-shot, 551 documents); only the active constraint layers differ across rows.\n\n% ──────────────────────────────────────────\n%  Table: SciERC five-way ablation\n% ──────────────────────────────────────────\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\setlength{\\tabcolsep}{3.6pt}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}l rrrrrr rr@{}}\n\\toprule\n& \\multicolumn{6}{c}{\\textbf{Violation Rates (\\%)}}\n& \\multicolumn{2}{c}{\\textbf{F1}} \\\\\n\\cmidrule(lr){2-7} \\cmidrule(l){8-9}\n\\textbf{Mode}\n  & DR\\,$\\downarrow$ & $\\Delta$DR\n  & RV\\,$\\downarrow$ & ET\\,$\\downarrow$ & CF & Tot.\\,$\\downarrow$\n  & Rel.\\,$\\uparrow$ & Ent.\\,$\\uparrow$ \\\\\n\\midrule\nUnconstrained\n  & 15.37 & ---\n  & 1.39  & 0.40\n  & 0.08  & 15.45\n  & 8.23  & 31.01 \\\\\nGrammar\n  & 14.84 & $\\downarrow$3\\%\n  & \\textbf{0} & \\textbf{0}\n  & 0.08  & 14.92\n  & 8.23  & 30.98 \\\\\nGCD-equiv.$^\\dagger$\n  & 15.26 & $\\downarrow$1\\%\n  & \\textbf{0} & \\textbf{0}\n  & 0.04  & 15.30\n  & 8.75  & 30.91 \\\\\nSemantic\n  & 11.65 & $\\downarrow$24\\%\n  & 1.51  & 0.40\n  & 2.71  & 14.36\n  & 8.15  & 31.01 \\\\\nS1\n  & \\textbf{0.93} & $\\downarrow$\\textbf{94\\%}\n  & \\textbf{0} & \\textbf{0}\n  & 9.90  & \\textbf{10.83}\n  & \\textbf{8.36} & 30.98 \\\\\nS2\n  & 0.97  & $\\downarrow$94\\%\n  & \\textbf{0} & \\textbf{0}\n  & 8.03  & \\textbf{9.00}\n  & 8.03  & \\textbf{31.90} \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}%\n\\caption{Five-way ablation on SciERC (551 docs, Qwen3-8B, zero-shot).\n$\\Delta$DR: relative change vs.\\ unconstrained; Tot.\\ = DR + CF; bold = best (CF excluded).\nF1 (\\%). S1/S2 = single/two-pass (\\S\\ref{sec:strategies}).\nGrammar = grammar-constrained decoding (GCD) ceiling~\\cite{geng2023gcd}.\n$\\dagger$vLLM \\texttt{guided\\_json}~\\cite{kwon2023vllm} (\\S\\ref{sec:constrained-baselines}).\nDeterministic (greedy, $T{=}$0); robustness evidence in Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model} and Appendices~\\ref{app:bootstrap-effect-sizes},~\\ref{app:prompt-sensitivity},~\\ref{app:doc-length}.}\n\\label{tab:scierc-ablation}\n\\end{table}\n\n% ──────────────────────────────────────────\n%  (a) Zero-shot diagnostic (compressed)\n% ──────────────────────────────────────────\n\\paragraph{Zero-shot diagnostic.}\n% phase2_scierc_unconstrained: DR=0.1537; phase2_scierc_full_s1: DR=0.0093\nTable~\\ref{tab:scierc-ablation} establishes that DR violations are pervasive (15--18\\% across datasets) and that constraint layers interact non-additively: grammar-only achieves 3\\% DR reduction on SciERC, semantic-only 24\\%, but their composition yields 94\\%---the \\emph{empirical enabling dependency}.\nOn DocRED (96 relations, $\\bar{\\kappa}{=}0.842$), grammar-only \\emph{increases} DR via \\textit{latent violation surfacing}: forcing out-of-vocabulary (OOV)$\\to$valid relation mapping exposes violations previously masked by vocabulary errors (Appendices~\\ref{app:cross-model-zs},~\\ref{app:docred-zs-ablation}).\nCross-model validation (3 models $\\times$ 2 datasets; Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model}) confirms DR drops of 76--99\\% on SciERC and 42--90\\% on DocRED, with R-F1 improving in 5/6 settings; post-hoc filtering achieves DR${=}$0\\% but at 15--74\\% rejection rates.\nDR-to-CF conversion represents a severity downgrade: DR is irrecoverable without re-extraction, while CF---individually valid triples with cross-triple inconsistency---is amenable to post-hoc resolution (type consistency reranking (TCR) eliminates all CF in our experiments, with bounded DR regression; Appendix~\\ref{app:tcr}).\nZero-shot evaluation thus establishes violation prevalence and constraint interaction; fine-tuning demonstrates practical elimination.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig_ablation_waterfall.pdf}\n\\caption{Non-additive layer interaction on SciERC (zero-shot). Grammar-only and semantic-only reductions (3\\% and 24\\%) compose to 94\\%, revealing an enabling dependency.}\n\\label{fig:ablation-waterfall}\n\\end{figure}\n\n% ──────────────────────────────────────────\n%  (e) Composability with Fine-Tuning (expanded)\n% ──────────────────────────────────────────\n\\paragraph{Composability with fine-tuning.}\n\\label{sec:composability}\n% exp_ids: bootstrap_ci_ft_composability.json, w5_lora_scierc_*, w8_*\nFine-tuning alone reduces DR to 0.5--2\\% but leaves residual violations on tight-constraint relations (Table~\\ref{tab:composability}); adding S1 yields DR\\,$\\leq$\\,0.21\\% with no significant $\\Delta$R-F1 (Bonferroni-corrected; 95\\% CIs $\\subseteq [-0.69, {+}0.25]$\\,pp; removed triples are 97.6--100\\% false positives; Appendix~\\ref{app:downstream-impact}).\nUnder fine-tuning, grammar becomes a no-op ($\\Delta$DR$<$0.2\\,pp), making the semantic layer the sole DR defense (41--69\\% on SciERC, 2--75\\% on DocRED; Appendix~\\ref{app:ft-ablation}); three relations with $\\kappa \\geq 0.36$ retain DR, all eliminated by S1 (Appendix~\\ref{app:lora-per-relation}).\nFallback frequency is model-dependent: Qwen triggers 0 fallbacks under LoRA + S1 on SciERC, while Llama triggers 5{,}882 (Table~\\ref{tab:ft-ablation}), reflecting weaker entity typing; despite this, DR reaches 0.09\\% on SciERC (0.21\\% on DocRED), confirming that even frequent fallback does not compromise enforcement (Appendix~\\ref{app:llama-per-relation}).\nCF drops from $+$9.82\\,pp (zero-shot) to $+$1.18\\,pp (fine-tuned), concentrated in \\textsc{Generic}$\\leftrightarrow$X ambiguity (63.3\\% of zero-shot CF; Appendix~\\ref{app:cf-distribution}); beam ($k{=}4$) eliminates all violation types at 8B (R-F1 43--46; \\S\\ref{sec:beam-results}).\n\n% ──────────────────────────────────────────\n%  CF quantification (MF1 response)\n% ──────────────────────────────────────────\n\\paragraph{Cross-field violation analysis.}\n% exp_ids: phase2_scierc_full_s1 (ZS: CF=248, 167 docs), w5_lora_scierc_full_s1_eval (FT: CF=21, 20 docs)\nUnder zero-shot greedy S1, CF is the sole residual violation type (DR, RV, ET all eliminated): 248 violations across 167 documents, 63.3\\% involving \\textsc{Generic}$\\leftrightarrow$X type ambiguity (Appendix~\\ref{app:cf-distribution}).\nA source breakdown shows 58.1\\% originate from hallucinated entities, while true cascade errors account for only 7.3\\% (Appendix~\\ref{app:cascade-analysis}); only the active constraint layers differ across rows.\n\n% ──────────────────────────────────────────\n%  Table: SciERC five-way ablation\n% ──────────────────────────────────────────\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\setlength{\\tabcolsep}{3.6pt}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}l rrrrrr rr@{}}\n\\toprule\n& \\multicolumn{6}{c}{\\textbf{Violation Rates (\\%)}}\n& \\multicolumn{2}{c}{\\textbf{F1}} \\\\\n\\cmidrule(lr){2-7} \\cmidrule(l){8-9}\n\\textbf{Mode}\n  & DR\\,$\\downarrow$ & $\\Delta$DR\n  & RV\\,$\\downarrow$ & ET\\,$\\downarrow$ & CF & Tot.\\,$\\downarrow$\n  & Rel.\\,$\\uparrow$ & Ent.\\,$\\uparrow$ \\\\\n\\midrule\nUnconstrained\n  & 15.37 & ---\n  & 1.39  & 0.40\n  & 0.08  & 15.45\n  & 8.23  & 31.01 \\\\\nGrammar\n  & 14.84 & $\\downarrow$3\\%\n  & \\textbf{0} & \\textbf{0}\n  & 0.08  & 14.92\n  & 8.23  & 30.98 \\\\\nGCD-equiv.$^\\dagger$\n  & 15.26 & $\\downarrow$1\\%\n  & \\textbf{0} & \\textbf{0}\n  & 0.04  & 15.30\n  & 8.75  & 30.91 \\\\\nSemantic\n  & 11.65 & $\\downarrow$24\\%\n  & 1.51  & 0.40\n  & 2.71  & 14.36\n  & 8.15  & 31.01 \\\\\nS1\n  & \\textbf{0.93} & $\\downarrow$\\textbf{94\\%}\n  & \\textbf{0} & \\textbf{0}\n  & 9.90  & \\textbf{10.83}\n  & \\textbf{8.36} & 30.98 \\\\\nS2\n  & 0.97  & $\\downarrow$94\\%\n  & \\textbf{0} & \\textbf{0}\n  & 8.03  & \\textbf{9.00}\n  & 8.03  & \\textbf{31.90} \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}%\n\\caption{Five-way ablation on SciERC (551 docs, Qwen3-8B, zero-shot).\n$\\Delta$DR: relative change vs.\\ unconstrained; Tot.\\ = DR + CF; bold = best (CF excluded).\nF1 (\\%). S1/S2 = single/two-pass (\\S\\ref{sec:strategies}).\nGrammar = grammar-constrained decoding (GCD) ceiling~\\cite{geng2023gcd}.\n$\\dagger$vLLM \\texttt{guided\\_json}~\\cite{kwon2023vllm} (\\S\\ref{sec:constrained-baselines}).\nDeterministic (greedy, $T{=}$0); robustness evidence in Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model} and Appendices~\\ref{app:bootstrap-effect-sizes},~\\ref{app:prompt-sensitivity},~\\ref{app:doc-length}.}\n\\label{tab:scierc-ablation}\n\\end{table}\n\n% ──────────────────────────────────────────\n%  (a) Zero-shot diagnostic (compressed)\n% ──────────────────────────────────────────\n\\paragraph{Zero-shot diagnostic.}\n% phase2_scierc_unconstrained: DR=0.1537; phase2_scierc_full_s1: DR=0.0093\nTable~\\ref{tab:scierc-ablation} establishes that DR violations are pervasive (15--18\\% across datasets) and that constraint layers interact non-additively: grammar-only achieves 3\\% DR reduction on SciERC, semantic-only 24\\%, but their composition yields 94\\%---the \\emph{empirical enabling dependency}.\nOn DocRED (96 relations, $\\bar{\\kappa}{=}0.842$), grammar-only \\emph{increases} DR via \\textit{latent violation surfacing}: forcing out-of-vocabulary (OOV)$\\to$valid relation mapping exposes violations previously masked by vocabulary errors (Appendices~\\ref{app:cross-model-zs},~\\ref{app:docred-zs-ablation}).\nCross-model validation (3 models $\\times$ 2 datasets; Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model}) confirms DR drops of 76--99\\% on SciERC and 42--90\\% on DocRED, with R-F1 improving in 5/6 settings; post-hoc filtering achieves DR${=}$0\\% but at 15--74\\% rejection rates.\nDR-to-CF conversion represents a severity downgrade: DR is irrecoverable without re-extraction, while CF---individually valid triples with cross-triple inconsistency---is amenable to post-hoc resolution (type consistency reranking (TCR) eliminates all CF in our experiments, with bounded DR regression; Appendix~\\ref{app:tcr}).\nZero-shot evaluation thus establishes violation prevalence and constraint interaction; fine-tuning demonstrates practical elimination.\n\n% ──────────────────────────────────────────\n%  (e) Composability with Fine-Tuning (expanded)\n% ──────────────────────────────────────────\n\\paragraph{Composability with fine-tuning.}\n\\label{sec:composability}\n% exp_ids: bootstrap_ci_ft_composability.json, w5_lora_scierc_*, w8_*\nFine-tuning alone reduces DR to 0.5--2\\% but leaves residual violations on tight-constraint relations (Table~\\ref{tab:composability}); adding S1 yields DR\\,$\\leq$\\,0.21\\% with no significant $\\Delta$R-F1 (Bonferroni-corrected; 95\\% CIs $\\subseteq [-0.69, {+}0.25]$\\,pp; removed triples are 97.6--100\\% false positives; Appendix~\\ref{app:downstream-impact}).\nUnder fine-tuning, grammar becomes a no-op ($\\Delta$DR$<$0.2\\,pp), making the semantic layer the sole DR defense (41--69\\% on SciERC, 2--75\\% on DocRED; Appendix~\\ref{app:ft-ablation}); three relations with $\\kappa \\geq 0.36$ retain DR, all eliminated by S1 (Appendix~\\ref{app:lora-per-relation}).\nFallback frequency is model-dependent: Qwen triggers 0 fallbacks under LoRA + S1 on SciERC, while Llama triggers 5{,}882 (Table~\\ref{tab:ft-ablation}), reflecting weaker entity typing; despite this, DR reaches 0.09\\% on SciERC (0.21\\% on DocRED), confirming that even frequent fallback does not compromise enforcement (Appendix~\\ref{app:llama-per-relation}).\nCF drops from $+$9.82\\,pp (zero-shot) to $+$1.18\\,pp (fine-tuned), concentrated in \\textsc{Generic}$\\leftrightarrow$X ambiguity (63.3\\% of zero-shot CF; Appendix~\\ref{app:cf-distribution}); beam ($k{=}4$) eliminates all violation types at 8B (R-F1 43--46; \\S\\ref{sec:beam-results}).\n\n% ──────────────────────────────────────────\n%  CF quantification (MF1 response)\n% ──────────────────────────────────────────\n\\paragraph{Cross-field violation analysis.}\n% exp_ids: phase2_scierc_full_s1 (ZS: CF=248, 167 docs), w5_lora_scierc_full_s1_eval (FT: CF=21, 20 docs)\nUnder zero-shot greedy S1, CF is the sole residual violation type (DR, RV, ET all eliminated): 248 violations across 167 documents, 63.3\\% involving \\textsc{Generic}$\\leftrightarrow$X type ambiguity (Appendix~\\ref{app:cf-distribution}).\nA source breakdown shows 58.1\\% originate from hallucinated entities, while true cascade errors account for only 7.3\\% (Appendix~\\ref{app:cascade-analysis}); fine-tuning reduces CF to 1.18\\% with cascade errors eliminated; beam $k{=}4$ eliminates all remaining CF (\\S\\ref{sec:beam-results}).\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\setlength{\\tabcolsep}{3pt}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}lll rrrr r@{}}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Model} & \\textbf{Dataset} & \\textbf{Mode}\n  & \\textbf{DR\\,$\\downarrow$} & \\textbf{CF}\n  & \\textbf{R-F1\\,$\\uparrow$} & \\textbf{E-F1\\,$\\uparrow$}\n  & \\textbf{$\\Delta$DR} \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multirow{4}{*}{Qwen3-8B}\n  & \\multirow{2}{*}{SciERC}\n    & Unconstr.\\ &  1.95 & 0    & 43.86 & 67.05 & --- \\\\\n  & & S1    & \\textbf{0} & 1.18 & 43.77 & 67.05 & $\\downarrow$100\\% \\\\\n\\cmidrule(l){2-8}\n  & \\multirow{2}{*}{DocRED}\n    & Unconstr.\\ &  0.76 & 0.12 & 40.86 & 82.15 & --- \\\\\n  & & S1    & 0.01 & 0.41 & 40.56 & 81.43 & $\\downarrow$98.7\\% \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multirow{4}{*}{\\shortstack[l]{Llama\\\\3.1-8B}}\n  & \\multirow{2}{*}{SciERC}\n    & Unconstr.\\ &  1.11 & 0    & 45.45 & 67.76 & --- \\\\\n  & & S1    &  0.09 & 0.46 & 45.47 & 67.76 & $\\downarrow$92\\% \\\\\n\\cmidrule(l){2-8}\n  & \\multirow{2}{*}{DocRED}\n    & Unconstr.\\ &  0.49 & 0.08 & 43.72 & 83.93 & --- \\\\\n  & & S1    &  0.21 & 0.21 & 43.78 & 83.94 & $\\downarrow$57\\% \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}%\n\\caption{Fine-tuning composability: LoRA $\\times$ unconstrained vs.\\ S1 on SciERC (551 docs) and DocRED (998 dev docs).\nF1 (\\%). No $\\Delta$R-F1 is significant (Bonferroni-corrected; details in text); all DR reductions are ($p{<}0.001$).\nE-F1 (entity F1) includes parse-failure documents (Qwen PF (parse failure): 7--8\\%; Llama PF: 2--3\\%).}\n\\label{tab:composability}\n\\end{table}\n\n% ──────────────────────────────────────────\n%  (f) Downstream impact of DR elimination\n%  exp_ids: task_e_lucky_violation_analysis\n% ──────────────────────────────────────────\n\\paragraph{Downstream impact.}\n\\label{sec:downstream-impact}\nConstraint removal affects only 0.24\\% of gold triples (Appendix~\\ref{app:lucky-violations}); error analysis of 514 false negatives confirms only one (0.2\\%) was blocked by constraints, with dominant sources (relation confusion, entity type polysemy) schema-valid and beyond constraint reach (Appendix~\\ref{app:error-analysis}).\nAcross fine-tuned settings, 36 residual DR violations decompose into entity type errors (39\\%), schema coverage gaps (33\\%), and relation/direction errors (28\\%); the irreducible floor is $\\sim$3 violations (Appendix~\\ref{app:residual-dr-analysis}).\n",

        "content": "% Runtime overhead (inline paragraph, no subsection header to save space)\n\n\\paragraph{Runtime overhead.}\n\\label{sec:runtime}\nS1 overhead is $+$13.1\\% on SciERC, lower on DocRED due to amortization over longer sequences; under LoRA, $+$10.0\\% with 37\\% lower absolute latency (Appendix~\\ref{app:ft-latency}).\nConstraint compilation is one-time $<$0.5\\,s; scaling benchmarks confirm constant overhead (2--3\\%) up to 200 entity types and 1{,}000 relations (Appendix~\\ref{app:scalability}).\n\n\\paragraph{Beam search compatibility.}\n\\label{sec:beam-results}\n% exp_id: d066_beam_sweep\nWith LoRA, beam search~\\cite{anderson2017guided} eliminates all four violation types at 8B on both SciERC and DocRED; at 70B, beam $k{=}4$ achieves DR${=}$RV${=}$ET${=}$0\\% with one residual CF on SciERC (Appendix~\\ref{app:beam-search}).\nA beam-width sweep ($k \\in \\{1,2,4,8\\}$) on SciERC reveals $k{=}2$ as Pareto-optimal: R-F1 44.60 (highest), CF 0.11\\%, at only 1.18$\\times$ greedy S1 latency; $k{=}4$ achieves CF${=}$0\\% but lower R-F1 (43.60) at 1.39$\\times$ (Figure~\\ref{fig:beam-pareto}; Table~\\ref{tab:beam-sweep} in Appendix~\\ref{app:beam-search}; latencies relative to greedy S1).\nThis yields three deployment tiers: greedy S1 ($+$10\\% latency, DR${\\leq}$0.21\\%, CF${\\leq}$1.4\\%), beam $k{=}2$ (1.18$\\times$, near-zero CF), and beam $k{=}4$ (1.39$\\times$, all violations${\\to}$0\\% at 8B).\nPer-beam memory overhead is $O(k \\times |\\text{entities}| \\times |\\text{types}|)$ for independent entity registry copies, negligible at typical document scales.\n\n% exp_ids: r16_70b_scierc_zs_s1, r16_70b_docred_zs_s1, 70b_lora_scierc, 70b_lora_docred_200\n\\paragraph{70B-class validation (single-family).}\n\\label{sec:70b-results}\nOn Llama-3.1-70B-Instruct (Activation-aware Weight Quantization (AWQ) INT4), zero-shot S1 eliminates DR on both datasets (SciERC 6.41\\%\\,$\\to$\\,0\\%, DocRED 14.98\\%\\,$\\to$\\,0\\%; R-F1 11--13).\nA four-way ablation reveals scale-dependent layer interaction: semantic-only achieves 84\\% DR reduction vs.\\ 8B's 24\\% (Table~\\ref{tab:scierc-ablation}), though grammar remains necessary for RV elimination and CF control (semantic-only CF${=}$76).\nLoRA confirms composability: DR\\,$\\to$\\,0\\% on both datasets (R-F1 44.53, 43.95; $|\\Delta| \\leq 0.20$\\,pp; CF 0.18--1.33\\%); beam ($k{=}4$) reduces DocRED parse failures (12.5\\%\\,$\\to$\\,0.5\\%, R-F1 $+$4.19\\,pp; Appendices~\\ref{app:70b-ablation},~\\ref{app:70b-docred-zs},~\\ref{app:70b-docred}).\nCross-family verification (e.g., Qwen-72B) remains future work; an 8B ablation confirms quantization does not affect enforcement (Appendix~\\ref{app:quantization}).\n\n% exp_ids: trie_qwen_ft_scierc, trie_qwen_zs_scierc, trie_llama_ft_scierc, trie_llama_zs_scierc\n\\paragraph{Comparison with grammar-constrained approaches.}\n\\label{sec:constrained-baselines}\nOur grammar-only ablation (Table~\\ref{tab:scierc-ablation}, row~2) implements the same constraints as GCD~\\cite{geng2023gcd} and BoostCD~\\cite{boostcd2025}; vLLM \\texttt{guided\\_json}~\\cite{kwon2023vllm} confirms equivalence (DR${=}$15.26\\%; Appendix~\\ref{app:gcd-comparison}).\nThe ${\\sim}$15\\% residual DR is the gap our semantic layer addresses (94\\% total reduction); trie-only constraints without JSON enforcement yield 9--51\\% parse failure (Appendix~\\ref{app:trie-baseline}).\n",

    "content": {

        "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_6/projects/schema_semantic_constrained_ie/artifacts/_project/paper/section7_conclusion_compressed.tex",
        "content": "% Section 7 — Discussion and Conclusion\n\nWe validated OntoCoDec across 2 RE datasets, 3 model families, and event extraction (\\S\\ref{sec:wikievents}).\nThe central finding is non-additive layer interaction: grammar alone achieves 3\\% DR reduction on SciERC, semantic alone 24\\%, but their composition yields 94\\% at 8B (\\S\\ref{sec:scierc-results})---grammar-enforced vocabulary membership creates the clean input the semantic layer requires.\n\n\\paragraph{Scale-dependent layer interaction.}\nThis dependency weakens at scale: at 70B, the semantic layer independently achieves 84\\% DR reduction (\\S\\ref{sec:70b-results}).\nWe offer a speculative hypothesis: larger models exhibit improved vocabulary calibration, assigning higher probability to ontology-valid tokens and reducing the semantic layer's dependence on grammar-enforced vocabulary membership (Appendix~\\ref{app:causal-tracing}); confirming this causal mechanism requires cross-family verification beyond our current scope.\nFallback frequency varies dramatically across architectures (0 vs.\\ 5{,}882 under LoRA+S1) without affecting enforcement (both DR\\,$\\leq$\\,0.21\\%), confirming grammar is necessary but increasingly insufficient as models improve.\nConstraint enforcement is preserved under INT4 quantization (Appendix~\\ref{app:quantization}).\n\n\\paragraph{DR$\\leftrightarrow$CF trade-off.}\nGreedy S1 reduces DR to $\\leq$\\,0.21\\% but introduces CF (zero-shot $+$9.82\\,pp, fine-tuned $+$1.18\\,pp)---an architectural consequence of per-triple independent enforcement.\nAmong 248 zero-shot CF violations, 58.1\\% involve hallucinated entities (absent from gold) and only 7.3\\% are true cascade errors (\\S\\ref{sec:scierc-results}); fine-tuned CF drops to $\\leq$1.4\\%.\nBeam $k{=}2$ reduces CF to 0.11\\% at 1.18$\\times$ latency (Pareto-optimal; recommended for zero-shot deployment); $k{=}4$ eliminates all violations at 8B at 1.39$\\times$ (\\S\\ref{sec:beam-results}).\n\n\\paragraph{Complementarity and deployment.}\nGoLLIE and KnowCoder internalize schema knowledge via training but lack runtime guarantees; LoRA + S1 shows the two are complementary (DR 1--2\\%\\,$\\to$\\,$\\leq$0.21\\%).\nS2 (two-pass) degrades at 70B ($-$8\\,pp R-F1), consistent with CRANE~\\cite{banerjee2025crane}; we recommend S1 as default.\nThe root cause---per-triple enforcement cannot coordinate cross-triple type assignments (Level~3; \\S\\ref{sec:decidability})---requires global entity-level consensus beyond online masking; beam search and TCR approximate this, with full integration as future work.\n",
        "numLines": 21,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 21
      }
    },
    "displayPath": "../../../artifacts/_project/paper/section7_conclusion_compressed.tex"
